# Paper and NeoForge Adapter Leaf Audit

Audited revision: `4b67bcd934a744ab880e26a459860ab2e992e1d2` (`main`, 2026-08-25).

## Completion update — 2026-08-25

This document preserves the exhaustive before-state inventory. Every `NOT LEAF` declaration and
every parity gap identified below has now been moved into a core concept owner, reduced to an
atomic native operation/observation, capability-gated, or deleted.

A fresh source review covered the resulting production adapters. The later naming cleanup reduced
them further to 76 Paper and 22 NeoForge files without changing the classifications below:

- platform modules contain no NotQuests manager classes or shared persistence/configuration policy;
- lifecycle, commands, GUI behavior, conversations, quest/objective state, messages, matching,
  scheduling decisions, and migration behavior are core-owned;
- shared platform capabilities are required methods, so Paper/NeoForge omissions fail compilation;
- 6.3 decoding is isolated in `core/migrations/v6_3_0`; current adapters only consume canonical
  core data;
- NeoForge protects the journal for all menu click modes and creative inventory packets, and both
  marker rendering modes are represented explicitly;
- matching policies such as enchantment ranges and nearby-entity radius checks live in their core
  or builtin concept owners.

The completed actionable checklist is [adapter-boundary-violations.md](adapter-boundary-violations.md).
Final verification is `./gradlew build` plus `git diff --check`.

## Scope and method

This audit covers every production Java source file under:

- `src/paper/src/main/java`
- `src/neoforge/src/main/java`

The declaration inventory was produced from the Java compiler AST, not from a regex. It includes
every source-declared constructor and method in top-level, nested, local, and anonymous types.
Compiler-generated record accessors, enum methods, and implicit constructors are not source code and
therefore are not listed as method rows. Files with no source-declared methods are still represented
in the file summary.

The authority for the classifications is `AGENTS.md`, especially **Core vs Adapter Boundary** and
**What Counts As An Adapter Leaf**. `CONTRIBUTING.md` still contains an older summary that says Paper
owns some command/configuration work; where that conflicts, the more specific current boundary in
`AGENTS.md` wins.

## Classification rubric

| Verdict | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **LEAF** | One atomic platform or external-plugin observation, effect, conversion, rendering operation, scheduling primitive, dependency-wiring step, or direct platform-event-to-core translation. It does not own NotQuests policy. |
| **NOT LEAF** | Owns or participates in orchestration, shared state, persistence policy, portable parsing/matching, command behavior or user-facing text, quest/objective/conversation/GUI policy, registry metadata, broad multi-step flows, or a fake/stub implementation. It could make Paper and NeoForge behavior diverge. |

Important consequences of that rubric:

- Platform-specific code is not automatically a leaf.
- A Paper-only integration may legitimately remain in Paper while still containing `NOT LEAF`
  methods. Its atomic calls into the external plugin are leaves; its NotQuests type definition,
  task text, matching policy, and orchestration are not.
- A command compiler is allowed in an adapter, but only its Brigadier translation is leaf code.
  Portable token, alias, flag, and suggestion policy is not.
- A renderer can be a leaf even when it takes many lines, provided core already made the behavior
  decision and the method only materializes it in a native platform API.
- `NOT LEAF` identifies a boundary or cohesion issue; it does not by itself prescribe “move this
  exact method unchanged into core.” Often the right fix is to move policy into its core concept and
  leave behind a smaller platform fact/effect.

## Boundary violation ranking

The detailed tables add a boundary rating and a junior-friendly recommendation to every declaration. Only `V1`, `V2`, and `GAP` are violations. `KEEP` means the method is not a leaf but is allowed to remain platform-local under the integration/vendor/capability exceptions in `AGENTS.md`.

| Rating | Meaning | Paper | NeoForge | Total | Recommended order |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| **V1** | Major violation: shared NotQuests behavior, state, or orchestration is in an adapter. | 79 | 26 | 105 | Fix first; move decisions/state/sequencing to a core concept owner. |
| **GAP** | Adapter capability is incomplete, silently stubbed, or uses an unsafe fallback. | 3 | 1 | 4 | Fix alongside V1; implement it or capability-gate/remove the feature. |
| **V2** | Moderate violation: portable parsing, formatting, validation, metadata, or helper policy is in an adapter. | 209 | 71 | 280 | Fix after V1; fold it into an existing core owner without creating tiny helper classes. |
| **KEEP** | Not a leaf, but legitimately platform-local integration, vendor, or capability-gated code. | 140 | 5 | 145 | Keep here; isolate or consolidate for readability rather than moving it to core. |
| **N/A** | Structural declaration, empty required callback, utility constructor, or method-free file. | 39 | 9 | 48 | No boundary migration; clean up only when it improves readability. |
| **PASS** | Compliant atomic platform leaf. | 699 | 391 | 1090 | Keep in the adapter. |

The rating is architectural priority, not estimated effort. A ten-line `V1` can be more important than a hundred-line `V2` because the `V1` can make Paper and NeoForge behave differently.

## Summary

| Module | Production files | Source declarations | LEAF | NOT LEAF | Declaration-free files |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Paper | 104 | 1166 | 699 | 467 | 3 |
| NeoForge | 21 | 503 | 391 | 112 | 0 |
| **Total** | **125** | **1669** | **1090** | **579** | **3** |

Overall, **65.3%** of source-declared methods/constructors are `LEAF`; **34.7%** are `NOT LEAF`. These percentages measure boundary shape, not code quality.

## High-signal findings

The detailed rows identify these recurring `NOT LEAF` clusters:

- **Bootstrap and lifecycle orchestration:** both entry points still contain multi-service start/stop flows; several platform lifecycle methods are empty phase stubs rather than atomic capabilities.
- **Portable command behavior in adapters:** primitive parsing, validation/error text, flag tokenization, placeholder/comma suggestions, aliases/path manipulation, and parts of branch-help flow remain in Paper and/or NeoForge instead of the core command framework.
- **Core pass-through and player lifecycle state in adapter facades:** registry/variable/text pass-throughs are wiring at best, while Paper player create/load/register flows are genuine shared-state orchestration.
- **Conversation display policy:** both renderers mix native message delivery with speaker reconstruction, delete-previous/history recording, replay, and option-command policy.
- **Paper event-owned behavior:** journal protection/distribution, armor-stand admin tools, fresh-brew tracking, some marker cadence, and combined interaction flows go beyond direct Bukkit-event translation.
- **Paper integration features:** registry definitions, task text, matching rules, enablement state machines, recurring indicator loops, escort orchestration, and attachment validation are not leaves even where their smallest external-plugin calls are.
- **NPC and packet subsystems:** atomic native lookup/mutation and packet conversion are leaves; adapter-owned attachment flows, replay filtering, capability-disable policy, and incomplete/no-op methods are not.
- **NeoForge client marker projection:** native geometry submission is a leaf, but target projection/range-placement decisions and pure clamp math belong with core marker policy.
- **Vendored bStats:** most of `Metrics.java` is a standalone telemetry/network/JSON subsystem, not a NotQuests platform leaf. This does not imply it belongs in core; it means it should not be counted as evidence that Paper is a thin adapter.

The clearest `LEAF` clusters are `PlatformPlayer` native observations/effects, item/location conversions, scheduler/thread primitives, native registry enumeration, GUI/beam/packet rendering, cancellable platform event emission, direct gameplay-event fact translation, and one-step external-plugin reads/writes.
